We can read off the Stokes constants from the entries of the matrices appearing in this factorisation.

6.3
The 3d index

In
[
20
]
, remarkable
q
-series invariants of three-manifolds were proposed. These were then shown invariant under certain 2–3 moves when the associated
q
-series were convergent. Using state integrals,
[
35
]
showed that this is a true topological invariant of the manifold. As with many three-manifold invariants, the 3d index is built from gluing together pieces associated to tetrahedra. This piece is the so called tetrahedral index and given in a certain basis by the
q
-series

\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}(m,e;q)=\sum_{n=\max(-e,0)}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}\frac{q^{n(n+1)/2-(n+\frac{1}{2}e)m}}{(q;q)_{n}(q;q)_{n+e}}.

Using Neumann–Zagier data of an ideal triangulation
[
62
]
of a manifold
M
that supports an angle structure
[
28
]
, the 3d index of
[
20
]
can be defined by taking

\mathcal{I}_{M}(m,e;q)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}}q^{\frac{1}{2}\nu\cdot k}\prod_{j=1}^{N}\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}((dm-be)_{j}-B_{j}\cdot k,(-cm+ae)_{j}+A_{j}\cdot k;q),

where
r
is the number of tetrahedra minus the number of cusps,
m,e\in\mathbb{Z}^{N-r}
,
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\in M_{N-r,N}(\mathbb{Z})
give the gluing equations for the cusps, and
A,B\in M_{r,N}(\mathbb{Z})
and
\nu\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}
give the gluing equations for the edges. It was conjectured
[
30
,
43
,
57
]
that the Stokes constants of asymptotic series associated to knots were related to the 3d index. It is natural to attempt to understand if a similar story could hold for closed
3
-manifolds.

Recently, a surgery formula was proposed for the 3d index
[
26
]
. This proposal does not enjoy a proof of topological invariance, unlike the 3d index for knots. However, there is some numerical evidence
[
26
]
of topological invariance. Using the computation of the Stokes constants from Section
6.2
and the conjecture of
[
30
, Conjecture 2]
, we have a natural guess for the 3d index of
4_{1}(-1,2)
given by

1+\mathsf{S}_{+}^{(\rho_{6},\rho_{6})}(q)=1-2q-3q^{2}+3q^{4}+10q^{5}+14q^{6}+22q^{7}+20q^{8}+14q^{9}+\cdots.

Therefore, we can compare
11
11

11

Upon posting the original version of this work on the arXiv, I received an email from Dongmin Gang with a question of whether the Stokes constants matched their 3d index, which for this manifold was given
1-2q-3q^{2}+3q^{4}+10q^{5}+14q^{6}+\cdots
.
with the proposal of
[
26
]
. For the knot
4_{1}
, we take
\alpha=[1,0]
,
\beta=[0,-1]
,
\gamma=[-1,0]
,
\delta=[1,0]
,
A=[-1,-1]
,
B=[1,1]
,
\nu=0
. We will use the notation of
[
26
]
, so that for
m,e\in\mathbb{Z}
and
[a,b;c,d]\in\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{SO}(3)}(m,e,c,d;q):=\frac{1}{2}(-1)^{am+2be}\bigl(\delta_{cm+2de,0}\bigl(q^{\frac{am+2be}{2}}-q^{-\frac{am+2be}{2}}\bigr)-\delta_{|cm+2de|,2}\bigr),

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{S^{3}\smallsetminus\mathbf{4_{1}}}^{(\mu,\lambda)}(m,e;q)=\sum_{e_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}(m-e_{1},m+e-e_{1};q)\mathcal{I}_{\Delta}(e-e_{1},-e_{1};q),

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{4_{1}(-1,2)}(q):=\sum_{m,e\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{SO}(3)}(m,e,-1,2;q)\mathcal{I}_{S^{3}\smallsetminus\mathbf{4_{1}}}^{(\mu,\lambda)}(m,e;q)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\mathcal{I}_{4_{1}(-1,2)}(q) \ }{}=1-2q-3q^{2}+3q^{4}+10q^{5}+14q^{6}+22q^{7}+20q^{8}+14q^{9}+\cdots.

As can be seen, this expansion agrees with the expansion of
1+\mathsf{S}_{+}^{(\rho_{6},\rho_{6})}(q)
where we recall that
\rho_{6}
is the geometric connection. This gives more numerical evidence for the proposals of both
[
26
,
30
]
.

Appendix A
Modularity of the
\boldsymbol{q}
-Pochhammer symbol

The
q
-Pochhammer symbol satisfies various modularity properties that it inherits from the Faddeev quantum dilogarithm. A fantastic description of Faddeev’s quantum dilogarithm is given in
[
3
, Appendix A]
however we will include some additional formulae that we will need. There are three different formulae for the Faddeev quantum dilogarithm on various domains. Using the notation
\tau=\mathsf{b}^{2}
,
c_{\mathsf{b}}=\frac{{\rm i}}{2}(\mathsf{b}+\mathsf{b}^{-1})
and the standard
q=\mathbf{e}(\tau)
and
\tilde{q}=\mathbf{e}(-1/\tau)
, we have (when defined)

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathsf{b}}(x)={}

\displaystyle\exp\biggl(\int_{\mathbb{R}+{\rm i}0}\frac{\exp(-2{\rm i}xw)}{4\sinh(\mathsf{b}w)\sinh\bigl(\mathsf{b}^{-1}w\bigr)}\frac{{\rm d}w}{w}\!\biggr)=\bigl(-q^{1/2}\exp(2\pi\mathsf{b}x);q\bigr)_{-\lfloor\operatorname{Im}(\mathsf{b}^{-1}x+{\rm i}\frac{\mathsf{b}^{-2}}{2})+\frac{1}{2}\rfloor}

\displaystyle\times\exp\biggl(\mathsf{b}^{2}\!\int_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}+0}\frac{\log\bigl(1+\tilde{q}^{-1/2}\mathbf{e}(-w)\exp(2\pi\mathsf{b}^{-1}x)\bigr)}{1-\mathbf{e}\bigl(-\mathsf{b}^{2}w\bigr)}{\rm d}w\biggr)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{\bigl(-q^{1/2}\exp(2\pi\mathsf{b}x);q\bigr)_{\infty}}{\bigl(-\tilde{q}^{1/2}\exp(2\pi\mathsf{b}^{-1}x);\tilde{q}\bigr)_{\infty}}.

These can be proved to be equivalent where their domains overlap by use of the residue theorem. For example, when
|\operatorname{Im}(x)|<\operatorname{Im}(c_{\mathsf{b}})
and
\operatorname{Im}(\tau)>0
the first and the last equation can be shown equal as

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}+{\rm i}0}\frac{\exp(-2{\rm i}xw)}{4\sinh(\mathsf{b}w)\sinh\bigl(\mathsf{b}^{-1}w\bigr)}\frac{{\rm d}w}{w}=-\int_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}+0}\frac{\mathbf{e}\bigl(-{\rm i}xw+\bigl(\mathsf{b}+\mathsf{b}^{-1}\bigr)w/2\bigr)}{(1-\mathbf{e}(\mathsf{b}w))\bigl(1-\mathbf{e}\bigl(\mathsf{b}^{-1}w\bigr)\bigr)}\frac{{\rm d}w}{w}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=2\pi{\rm i}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\bigl(\mathrm{Res}_{w=k\mathsf{b}}+\mathrm{Res}_{w=k\mathsf{b}^{-1}}\bigr)\frac{\mathbf{e}\bigl(-{\rm i}xw+\bigl(\mathsf{b}+\mathsf{b}^{-1}\bigr)w/2\bigr)}{(1-\mathbf{e}(\mathsf{b}w))\bigl(1-\mathbf{e}\bigl(\mathsf{b}^{-1}w\bigr)\bigr)}\frac{{\rm d}w}{w}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\tilde{q}^{k/2}\frac{\mathbf{e}\bigl(-k{\rm i}x\mathsf{b}^{-1}\bigr)}{k(1-\tilde{q}^{k})}-(-1)^{k}q^{k/2}\frac{\mathbf{e}(-k{\rm i}x\mathsf{b})}{k(1-q^{k})}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=-\log\bigl(-\tilde{q}^{1/2}\exp(2\pi\mathsf{b}^{-1}x);\tilde{q}\bigr)_{\infty}+\log\bigl(-q^{1/2}\exp(2\pi\mathsf{b}x);q\bigr)_{\infty},

where the last line follows from the equation
[
80
, Proposition 2]

-\log(x;q)_{\infty}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{x^{k}}{k(1-q^{k})}.

Then for the second and last when
\operatorname{Re}\bigl(\mathsf{b}^{2}\bigr)>0
and
\operatorname{Im}\bigl(\mathsf{b}^{2}\bigr)>0
, we use the integral for
n\in\mathbb{Z}

-\int_{-i\mathsf{b}^{-1}x+\frac{\mathsf{b}^{-2}}{2}-\frac{1}{2}-n}^{{\rm i}\infty}\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{1-\mathbf{e}\bigl(-\mathsf{b}^{2}w\bigr)}{\rm d}w=\mathsf{b}^{-2}\log\bigl(1+q^{1/2+n}\exp(2\pi\mathsf{b}x)\bigr).

We see that for the principle branch of the logarithm

\displaystyle\mathsf{b}^{2}\int_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}+0}\frac{\log\bigl(1+\tilde{q}^{-1/2}\mathbf{e}(-w)\exp(2\pi\mathsf{b}^{-1}x)\bigl)}{1-\mathbf{e}(-\mathsf{b}^{2}w)}{\rm d}w

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\mathrm{Res}_{w=k\mathsf{b}^{-2}}\frac{\log\bigl(1+\tilde{q}^{-1/2}\mathbf{e}(-w)\exp(2\pi\mathsf{b}^{-1}x)\bigr)}{1-\mathbf{e}\bigl(-\mathsf{b}^{2}w\bigr)}\frac{{\rm d}w}{\mathsf{b}^{-2}}

\displaystyle\qquad{}-\sum_{n=-\lfloor\operatorname{Im}(\mathsf{b}^{-1}x+{\rm i}\frac{\mathsf{b}^{-2}}{2})+\frac{1}{2}\rfloor}^{\infty}\int_{-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}^{-1}x+\frac{\mathsf{b}^{-2}}{2}-\frac{1}{2}-n}^{{\rm i}\infty}\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{1-\mathbf{e}\bigl(-\mathsf{b}^{2}w\bigr)}\frac{{\rm d}w}{\mathsf{b}^{-2}}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\log\bigl(1+\tilde{q}^{1/2+k}\exp\bigl(2\pi\mathsf{b}^{-1}x\bigr)\bigr)+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\log\bigl(1+q^{1/2+n}\exp(2\pi\mathsf{b}x)\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad{}-\sum_{n=0}^{-\lceil\operatorname{Im}(\mathsf{b}^{-1}x+{\rm i}\frac{\mathsf{b}^{-2}}{2})+\frac{1}{2}\rceil}\log\bigl(1+q^{1/2+n}\exp(2\pi\mathsf{b}x)\bigr)

\displaystyle=-\log\bigl(-\tilde{q}^{1/2}\exp(2\pi\mathsf{b}^{-1}x);\tilde{q}\bigr)_{\infty}+\log\bigl(-q^{1/2}\exp(2\pi\mathsf{b}x);q\bigr)_{\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad{}-\log\bigl(-q^{1/2}\exp(2\pi\mathsf{b}x);q\bigr)_{-\lfloor\operatorname{Im}(\mathsf{b}^{-1}x+{\rm i}\frac{\mathsf{b}^{-2}}{2})+\frac{1}{2}\rfloor}.

These various descriptions can be used to show that
\Phi_{\mathsf{b}}(x)
extends to a meromorphic function for
\mathsf{b}^{2}\in\mathbb{C}\smallsetminus\mathbb{R}_{\leq 0}
with simple poles and zeros determined by

\Phi_{\mathsf{b}}(x)^{\pm}=0\qquad\text{if and only if}\qquad x\in\mp(c_{\mathsf{b}}+{\rm i}\mathsf{b}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}+{\rm i}\mathsf{b}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}).

Therefore, for
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\operatorname{Im}\bigl(\mathsf{b}^{2}\bigr)>0
we have

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathsf{b}}\biggl({\rm i}\biggl(\frac{1}{2}\biggr)\mathsf{b}+{\rm i}\biggl(-k-\frac{1}{2}\biggr)\mathsf{b}^{-1}\biggr)\Phi_{\mathsf{b}}\biggl({\rm i}\biggl(\frac{1}{2}\biggr)\mathsf{b}+{\rm i}\biggl(-\frac{1}{2}\biggr)\mathsf{b}^{-1}\biggr)^{-1}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{(q;q)_{\infty}}{\bigl(\tilde{q}^{k+1};\tilde{q}\bigr)_{\infty}}\frac{(\tilde{q};\tilde{q})_{\infty}}{(q;q)_{\infty}}=(\tilde{q};\tilde{q})_{k}

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathsf{b}(q;q)_{\lfloor\operatorname{Re}(\mathsf{b}^{-2})k\rfloor}\exp\biggl(\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{24\mathsf{b}^{2}}-\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{4}+\frac{2\pi{\rm i}\mathsf{b}^{2}}{24}+\mathsf{b}^{2}\int_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}+0}\frac{\log\bigl(1-\tilde{q}^{k}\mathbf{e}(-w)\bigr)}{1-\mathbf{e}\bigl(-\mathsf{b}^{2}w\bigr)}{\rm d}w\biggr),

and given the analyticity of the quantum dilogarithm and the Pochhammer symbols the first and second line hold for all
\mathsf{b}^{2}\in\mathbb{C}\smallsetminus\mathbb{R}_{\leq 0}
. Therefore, let

f(k;\tau)=\frac{\tau}{24}-\frac{1}{8}+\frac{1}{24\tau}+\frac{\tau}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}+0}\frac{\log(1-\mathbf{e}(-k/\tau-w))}{1-\mathbf{e}(-\tau w)}{\rm d}w.

(A.1)

This function is meromorphic in
k
and
\tau
for
\operatorname{Re}(k/\tau)\notin\mathbb{Z}
and satisfies the periodicity

f(k+\tau;\tau)=f(k;\tau).

(A.2)

Then we can write

(\tilde{q};\tilde{q})_{k}=(q;q)_{\lfloor\operatorname{Re}(k/\tau)\rfloor}\mathbf{e}(f(k;\tau)).

(A.3)

We can determine the asymptotics of
f
by considering the equation

\displaystyle\frac{\tau}{2\pi{\rm i}}\!\int_{{\rm i}\mathbb{R}+0}\frac{\log(1-\mathbf{e}(-k/\tau-w))}{1-\mathbf{e}(-\tau w)}{\rm d}w=\frac{\tau}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}+{\rm i}0}\frac{\log(1-\exp(-2\pi{\rm i}k/\tau+2\pi w))}{1-\exp(2\pi\tau w)}{\rm d}w

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau}{2\pi}\int_{0+{\rm i}0}^{\infty}\frac{\log(1-\exp(-2\pi{\rm i}k/\tau+2\pi w))}{1-\exp(2\pi\tau w)}{\rm d}w

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\tau}{2\pi}\int_{0-i0}^{\infty}\frac{\log(1-\exp(-2\pi{\rm i}k/\tau-2\pi w))}{1-\exp(-2\pi\tau w)}{\rm d}w

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\log(1-\exp(-2\pi{\rm i}k/\tau-2\pi w)){\rm d}w+\frac{1}{4\pi i}\log(1-\exp(-2\pi{\rm i}k/\tau))

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\tau}{2\pi}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\log(1-\exp(-2\pi{\rm i}k/\tau+2\pi w))-\log(1-\exp(-2\pi{\rm i}k/\tau-2\pi w))}{1-\exp(2\pi\tau w)}{\rm d}w

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}\operatorname{Li}_{2}(\mathbf{e}(-k/\tau))+\frac{1}{2(2\pi{\rm i})}\log(1-\exp(-2\pi{\rm i}k/\tau))

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{{\rm i}\tau}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\log(1-\exp(-2\pi{\rm i}k/\tau+2\pi w))-\log(1-\exp(-2\pi{\rm i}k/\tau-2\pi w))}{1-\exp(2\pi\tau w)}{\rm d}w.

This can be used to show that

f(k\tau,\tau)=\frac{\tau}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}\operatorname{Li}_{2}(\mathbf{e}(-k))+\frac{\tau}{24}+O\bigl(\tau^{0}\bigr).

(A.4)

Appendix B
Asymptotic methods

In this appendix, we will provide more details on the proofs of the various theorems on asymptotics. In particular we will give more details on Theorems
3.3
and
4.1
. In upcoming work with Fantini, more refined methods are developed, which allow for even stronger results so we only outline these older methods.

B.1
Outline of the proof of Theorem
3.3

In this appendix, we will gives a few details on the proof of Theorem
3.3
.
This involves very similar arguments as the next Appendix
B.2
.
For
\tilde{q}=\mathbf{e}(i/r)
for
r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, we can rewrite the sum

\sum_{0\leq k\leq\ell}(-1)^{k+\ell}\frac{\tilde{q}^{\frac{1}{2}3k(k+1)+\frac{1}{2}\ell(\ell+1)-k}(\tilde{q};\tilde{q})_{\ell}}{(\tilde{q};\tilde{q})_{2k}(\tilde{q};\tilde{q})_{\ell-k}}=\sum_{k,j=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{j}\frac{\tilde{q}^{k(2k-1)+jk+\frac{1}{2}j(j+1)}(\tilde{q};\tilde{q})_{k+j}}{(\tilde{q};\tilde{q})_{2k}(\tilde{q};\tilde{q})_{j}}

with the Faddeev quantum dilogarithm and explicitly
f
from equation (
A.1
)

\displaystyle\sum_{k,j=0}^{\infty}\exp\biggl(-\frac{2\pi}{r}\biggl(\frac{{\rm i}}{2}rj+k(2k-1)+jk+\frac{1}{2}j(j+1)

\displaystyle\qquad{}-r{\rm i}f(k+j;{\rm i}/r)+r{\rm i}f(2k;{\rm i}/r)+r{\rm i}f(j;{\rm i}/r)\biggr)\biggr).

Notice that when
k,j>2r
the summand is
\mathrm{O}(\exp(-2\pi r))
. Therefore, this sum is asymptotic, up to
\mathrm{O}(\exp(-2\pi r))
, to

\displaystyle\sum_{x,y\in\frac{1}{r}\mathbb{Z}\cap[0,2]^{2}}\exp\biggl(-2\pi\biggl(\frac{{\rm i}}{2}ry+x(2xr-1)+yxr+\frac{1}{2}y(yr+1)

\displaystyle\qquad{}-{\rm i}f(xr+yr;{\rm i}/r)+{\rm i}f(2xr;{\rm i}/r)+{\rm i}f(yr;{\rm i}/r)\biggr)\biggr).

Using Abel–Plana summation, this sum is asymptotic to an integral with an appropriate parametrisation

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}\int_{0}^{2}\exp\biggl(-2\pi\biggl(\frac{{\rm i}}{2}ry+x(2xr-1)+yxr+\frac{1}{2}y(yr+1)

\displaystyle\qquad{}-{\rm i}f(xr+yr;{\rm i}/r)+{\rm i}f(2xr;{\rm i}/r)+{\rm i}f(yr;{\rm i}/r)\biggr)\biggr){\rm d}x{\rm d}y.

Therefore, we are interested in the behaviour of the function

\displaystyle W(x,y)={}

\displaystyle 2x^{2}+yx+\frac{1}{2}y^{2}-\frac{1}{2}y-\frac{1}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}\operatorname{Li}_{2}(\mathbf{e}(-x-y))+\frac{1}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}\operatorname{Li}_{2}(\mathbf{e}(-2x))

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}\operatorname{Li}_{2}(\mathbf{e}(-y))+\frac{1}{24}.

This has one critical point on this branch given by
(x_{3},y_{3})=(0.0081371{\rm i},-0.017231{\rm i})
.
One can show that on the set
x\in[0,1]

\operatorname{Re}\biggl(\frac{1}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}\operatorname{Li}_{2}(\mathbf{e}(-x))\biggr)

attains one maximum at
x=1/2
given by

\operatorname{Re}\biggl(\frac{1}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}\operatorname{Li}_{2}(-1)\biggr)=\frac{1}{48},

with minima at
x=0,1
given by

\operatorname{Re}\biggl(\frac{1}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}\operatorname{Li}_{2}(1)\biggr)=-\frac{1}{24}.

With this, we see that for
x,y\in[0,2]

W(x,y)\geq 2x^{2}+yx-\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{48}-\frac{1}{24}-\frac{1}{24}+\frac{1}{24},

and for
y>1.25
, we have

W(x,y)\geq 0.

Therefore, taking a parametrisation with end points beginning in the region where
W
is greater than or equal to zero and using Fubini’s theorem, we can deform the contour by the steepest descent of
W
fixing the real parts of
x
,
y
.
In the region where
W(x,y)\geq W(x_{3},y_{3})=0.0029434\ldots=\bigl(\mathrm{V}_{\rho_{3}}-4\pi^{2}\bigr)/(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}
. This deformation then passes through the critical point
(x_{3},y_{3})
where the integrand attains its maximum.
Indeed, all possible critical points are located at
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
translates of the following numbers and besides
(x_{3},y_{3})
they do not contribute:

\displaystyle(0.00813711\dots i,-0.0172319\dots i),

\displaystyle(0.5+0.350548\dots i,0.5-0.141931\dots i),

\displaystyle(-0.199662\dots i,0.5+0.0835868\dots i),

\displaystyle(0.5-0.042154\dots i,0.5+0.146002\dots i),

\displaystyle(-0.111324\dots i,0.5-0.0478456\dots i),

\displaystyle(0.336754\ldots-0.00277186\dots i,0.108538\ldots-0.0112903\dots i),

\displaystyle(-0.336754\ldots-0.00277186\dots i,-0.108538-0.0112903\dots i).

B.2
More details on Theorem
4.1

In this appendix, we will provide some more details on the proof of Theorem
4.1
. These methods are well known and see
[
63
]
for another relevant example of these methods. We wish to apply a stationary phase approximation to the integral of equation (
4.10
). The integrand has exponential behaviour as
r\to\infty
and the behaviour is determined by the function
V_{\alpha,\beta}
of equation (
4.4
). The size of the growth is determined by
\operatorname{Im}(V_{\alpha,\beta})
. To begin with, we want to understand the regions where the size of
\operatorname{Im}(V_{\alpha,\beta})
is larger than
\operatorname{Im}\bigl(-\frac{V_{\rho_{6}}}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}\bigr)
. This is depicted in Figure
7
and we prove explicitly this is true on the green regions.

C
D
A
B

Figure 7:
The regions
R
that arise from the stationary phase approximation. In
red
we have numerically computed the regions where
\operatorname{Im}(V_{\alpha,\beta})
is larger than
\operatorname{Im}\bigl(-\frac{V_{\rho_{6}}}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}\bigr)
and in
green
is the region with an explicit proof that it is smaller given in Lemma
B.1
. The four regions
A
,
B
,
C
,
D
give the connected components of the complement of the set
U
depicted in green. Note the very small red bumps on the boundary in the regions
C
and
D
.

Lemma B.1
.

The region
U
depicted in green in Figure
7
given by

U=\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c|l@{}}&2x+2y\in[0.05,0.33]+\mathbb{Z}\quad\text{or}\\
&x\in[0.67000,0.95000]+\mathbb{Z}\quad\text{or}\\
&x,y\in[0,0.025]\quad\text{or}\\
&x\in[0.95,1],\quad y\in[0,0.025]\quad\text{or}\\
(x,y)\in[0,1]&x\in[0,0.025],\quad y\in[0.95,1]\quad\text{or}\\
&x\in[0.95,1],\quad y\in[0.025,0.075],\quad 2x+2y\leq 2.05\quad\text{or}\\
&x,y\in[0.49250,1]\quad\text{or}\\
&x\in[0.5,1],\quad 2x+2y\in[-0.15,0.5]+\mathbb{Z}\quad\text{or}\\
&y\in[0.5,1],\quad 2x+2y\in[-0.15,0.5]+\mathbb{Z}\end{array}\right\}

satisfies the property that for
(x,y)\in U

\operatorname{Im}\biggl(\frac{V_{-1,1}(x,y)}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}+\frac{V_{\rho_{6}}}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}\biggr)>0.

Proof.

Firstly, we have

-\frac{V_{\rho_{6}}}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}=0.12330-0.035425{\rm i}.

Let

D(x)=\operatorname{Im}\biggl(\frac{\operatorname{Li}_{2}(\mathbf{e}(-x))}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}\biggr).

Then

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}x}D(x)=-\frac{\log(2\sin(\pi x))}{2\pi},\qquad\frac{{\rm d}^{2}}{{\rm d}x^{2}}D(z)=-\frac{\cot(\pi x)}{2}

has two stationary points at
1/6,5/6
for
x\in[0,1]
and achieves maximum and minimum values

\operatorname{Im}(\operatorname{Li}_{2}(\mathbf{e}(\pm 1/6))=\pm 1.0149\dots.

Therefore, one can compute

\displaystyle D(0.05)+0.035425=0.052608\ldots>0.051418\ldots=D(1/6),

\displaystyle D(0.33)+0.035425=0.052854\ldots>0.051418\ldots=D(1/6).

Therefore, for
2x+2y\in[0.05,0.33]+\mathbb{Z}
or
x,y\in[0.67000,0.95000]
, we see that

D(2x+2y)-D(x)-D(y)>-0.035425.

For
x,y\in[0,0.025]
, we see that

D(2x+2y)-D(x)-D(y)+0.035425>0.035425-2D(0.025)=0.012735.

For
x\in[0.95,1]
and
y\in[0,0.025]
, we see that

D(2x+2y)-D(x)-D(y)+0.035425>0.035425-D(0.1)-D(0.025)=0.00068087.

For
x\in[0.95,1]
,
y\in[0.025,0.075]
and
2x+2y\leq 2.05
, we have

D(2x+2y)-D(x)-D(y)+0.035425>0.035425-D(0.0500)-D(0.025)=0.0068973.

For
x,y\in[0.49250,1]
, we see that

\displaystyle D(2x+2y)-D(x)-D(y)+0.035425>0.035425-D(1/6)-2D(0.0075)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=3.5615\times 10^{-5}.

If
y\in[0.5,1]
and
2x+2y\in[-0.15,0.5]+\mathbb{Z}
, then

\displaystyle D(2x+2y)-D(x)-D(y)+0.035425>0.035425-D(1/6)=0.0097162.

∎

Let four regions
A
,
B
,
C
,
D
give the connected components of the complement of the set
U
depicted in green.

Lemma B.2
.

The integral of equation (
4.10
) with
\alpha=-1
,
\beta=1
over the region
B
,
C
, or
D
are
o\bigl(-V_{\rho_{6}}r/(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}\bigr)
.

Proof.

We will prove this by illustrating that the contours can be explicitly deformed,

\displaystyle V_{-1,1}(x,y)={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}\operatorname{Li}_{2}(\mathbf{e}(-2x-2y))-\frac{1}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}\operatorname{Li}_{2}(\mathbf{e}(-x))-\frac{1}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}\operatorname{Li}_{2}(\mathbf{e}(-y))

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{24}+\frac{1}{2}x^{2}+xy-\frac{1}{2}y^{2}-\frac{1}{2}x+\frac{1}{2}y.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V_{-1,1}}{\partial x}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{2\pi{\rm i}}\log(1-\mathbf{e}(-2x-2y))-\frac{1}{2\pi{\rm i}}\log(1-\mathbf{e}(-y))+x+y-\frac{1}{2},

\displaystyle\frac{\partial V_{-1,1}}{\partial y}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{2\pi{\rm i}}\log(1-\mathbf{e}(-2x-2y))-\frac{1}{2\pi{\rm i}}\log(1-\mathbf{e}(-x))+x-y+\frac{1}{2}.

Firstly, we will check the conditions for region
B
.
We see that

\operatorname{Im}\biggl({\rm i}\frac{\partial V_{-1,1}}{\partial y}-{\rm i}\frac{\partial V_{-1,1}}{\partial x}\biggr)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\arg(1-\mathbf{e}(-x)))-\frac{1}{2\pi}\arg(1-\mathbf{e}(-y)))-2\operatorname{Re}(y)+1.

For
\operatorname{Re}(x),\operatorname{Re}(y)\in(0,1/2)
,
\operatorname{Im}(x)\leq 0
, and
\operatorname{Im}(y)\geq 0
, we find that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\pi}\arg(1-\mathbf{e}(-x))),

\displaystyle-1

\displaystyle<-\frac{1}{2\pi}\arg(1-\mathbf{e}(-y)))-2\operatorname{Re}(y).

Therefore, for these conditions
x,y\in[0,1/2]
deforming the contour in the direction the
(-{\rm i},{\rm i})
direction will always increase the imaginary part of
V_{-1,1}(x,y)
, and therefore we see that the integral over the region
B
can be deformed to an integral that is
o(-V_{\rho_{6}}r/(2\pi{\rm i})^{2})
.

Secondly, for region
C
, we find for
\operatorname{Re}(x)\in[0.05,1/2)
and
\operatorname{Im}(x)\geq 0

-\frac{1}{2\pi}\arg(1-\mathbf{e}(-x)))\geq-0.45

Moreover, for
y\in[0.95,1)
and
\operatorname{Re}(x)\in[0.05,1/2)
and
\operatorname{Im}(x)\geq 0
, we find

\frac{2}{2\pi}\arg(1-\mathbf{e}(-2x-2y)))>0,

therefore, we find

\operatorname{Im}\biggl({\rm i}\frac{\partial V}{\partial x}\biggr)=\frac{2}{2\pi}\arg(1-\mathbf{e}(-2x-2y)))-\frac{1}{2\pi}\arg(1-\mathbf{e}(-x)))+\operatorname{Re}(x)+y-\frac{1}{2}\geq 0.05.

This implies that flowing a contour near the region
C
by increasing the imaginary part of
x
in this region will always increase the imaginary part of
V_{-1,1}
. Therefore, we see that we can deform the contour in this direction.
Therefore, for large enough
\operatorname{Im}(x)
, we see this will have positive imaginary part hence this contributes nothing to the asymptotics. Almost identical computation go through for the region
D
with
x
and
y
interchanged.
∎

Lemma B.3
.

The integral of equation (
4.10
) with
\alpha=-1
,
\beta=1
over the region
A
can be computed by stationary phase approximation at the critical point
(x_{6},y_{6})
.

Proof.

We deform the contour over region
A
by steepest ascent with respect to the function
\operatorname{Im}(V_{-1,1}(x,y))
fixing the real parts of
(x,y)
. In that region, there is only one critical point with real part contained in
A
given by
(x_{6},y_{6})
. Therefore, on deforming the contour, we find that is passes through the critical point, where it attains it minimum value given by
\operatorname{Im}\bigl(-\frac{V_{\rho_{6}}}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}}\bigr)
. Therefore, we can apply stationary phase to the integral of equation (
4.10
) at this point.
∎

Appendix C
Details of state integral factorisations

To deal with the state integrals, we use the notation

\Phi(z;\tau)=\Phi_{\mathsf{b}}\bigl({\rm i}z\mathsf{b}^{-1}+c_{\mathsf{b}}\bigr)=\frac{(q\mathbf{e}(z);q)_{\infty}}{(\mathbf{e}(z/\tau);\tilde{q})_{\infty}}.

Recall that for a positive integer
\ell
, the Eisenstein series are defined by

\displaystyle E_{\ell}(q)=\frac{\zeta(1-\ell)}{2}+\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}s^{\ell-1}\frac{q^{s}}{1-q^{s}},

(C.1)

where
\zeta(s)
is the Riemann zeta function, analytic for
|q|<1
and extended to
|q|>1
by the symmetry
E_{\ell}\bigl(q^{-1}\bigr)=-E_{\ell}(q)
. These Eisenstein series can be used with polylogarithms to describe an expansion of the infinite Pochhammer symbol.

Lemma C.1

(
[
34
,
41
]
)
.

We have identities in
\mathbb{Q}[\![q,\epsilon]\!]
, where we use
\bigl(q^{-1};q^{-1}\bigr)_{\infty}=(q;q)_{\infty}^{-1}
and
\bigl(x;q^{-1}\bigr)_{\infty}=(qx;q)_{\infty}^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\frac{(qe^{\varepsilon};q)_{\infty}}{(q;q)_{\infty}}\frac{1}{(qe^{\varepsilon};q)_{m}}=\frac{1}{(q;q)_{m}}\sqrt{\frac{-\varepsilon}{1-\exp(\varepsilon)}}\exp\left(-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(E_{\ell}(q)-\sum_{n=1}^{m}\operatorname{Li}_{1-\ell}(q^{n})\right)\frac{\varepsilon^{\ell}}{\ell!}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{\bigl(q^{-1}e^{\varepsilon};q^{-1}\bigr)_{\infty}}{\bigl(q^{-1};q^{-1}\bigr)_{\infty}}\frac{1}{\bigl(q^{-1}e^{\varepsilon};q^{-1}\bigr)_{m}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{\bigl(q^{-1};q^{-1}\bigr)_{m}}\frac{-1}{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\frac{-\varepsilon}{1-\exp(\varepsilon)}}\exp\left(-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(E_{\ell}\bigl(q^{-1}\bigr)-\sum_{n=1}^{m}\operatorname{Li}_{1-\ell}(q^{-n})\right)\frac{\varepsilon^{\ell}}{\ell!}\right).

C.1
At rationals

We can take the formula for the WRT invariant

\Sha_{m}(q)=\sum_{0\leq\ell\leq k}(-1)^{k}q^{-\frac{1}{2}k(k+1)+\ell(\ell+1)+mk}\frac{(q;q)_{2k+1}}{(q;q)_{\ell}(q;q)_{k-\ell}},

and naturally take the state integral

\int_{-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}(\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0)}\int_{-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}(\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0)}\frac{\mathbf{e}(-z_{1}(z_{1}+1+\tau)/2\tau+z_{3}(z_{3}+1+\tau)/\tau+z_{1}(m+m^{\prime}/\tau))}{\Phi(z_{3};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(z_{1}-z_{3};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(2z_{1}+1+\tau;\tau)}{\rm d}z_{1}{\rm d}z_{3}.

We can rewrite this integral by first sending
z_{1}\mapsto-z_{1}
,
z_{3}\mapsto-z_{3}
and using the inversion relation for the quantum dilogarithm
[
3
, Appendix A]
,

\displaystyle q^{-1-m}\tilde{q}^{1+m^{\prime}}

(C.2)

\displaystyle\times\iint_{(-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}(\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0))^{2}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\frac{\mathbf{e}(3z_{1}(z_{1}+1+\tau)/2\tau+z_{3}(z_{3}+1+\tau)/2\tau-z_{1}(m+1+(m^{\prime}+1)/\tau))}{\Phi(z_{3}-z_{1};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(2z_{1};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(z_{3};\tau)}{\rm d}z_{1}{\rm d}z_{3}.

Suppose that
\tau=N/M\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
. Then we can use the fundamental lemma of
[
33
, Lemma 2.1]
to factorise this state integral. However, we can use the Fourier transform formula for the quantum dilogarithm
[
3
, Appendix A]
to expand the Faddeev dilogarithm in
\Phi(z_{3};\tau)
, and then again to contract the integral over
z_{3}
. With these steps, one can show that this integral is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{m,m^{\prime}}=q^{-m}\tilde{q}^{m^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\times\int_{-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}(\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0)}\int_{-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}(\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0)}\frac{\mathbf{e}\bigl(z_{1}^{2}/\tau+z_{1}z_{2}/\tau+z_{1}(-m-m^{\prime}/\tau)+z_{2}(1+1/\tau)\bigr)}{\Phi(z_{2};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(2z_{1};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(z_{2}-z_{1};\tau)^{-1}}{\rm d}z_{1}{\rm d}z_{2}.

This integral expression has various benefits and in particular has a direction at infinity where the two dimensional contour can be pushed with vanishing contribution. However, we do not need this for the factorisation at rationals. We can explicitly use the fundamental lemma of
[
33
, Lemma 2.1]
to factorise this state integral. This gives the following lemma analogous to Lemma
5.1
but for
\tau\in\mathbb{Q}
. Lemma
5.3
also holds for
\tau\in\mathbb{Q}
and together they give Theorem
5.4
for
\tau\in\mathbb{Q}
.

Lemma C.2
.

We have the following identity for
q=\mathbf{e}(\tau)
,
\tilde{q}=\mathbf{e}(-1/\tau)
with
\tau=N/M
with coprime
M,N\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\mathcal{S}_{m,m^{\prime}}(\tau)=\sum_{j=1}^{7}\mathbf{e}\biggl(-\frac{\mathrm{V}_{\rho_{j}}}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}MN}\biggr)\Sha^{(j)}_{m}(q)\Sha^{(j)}_{m^{\prime}}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr).

(C.3)

Proof.

Indeed, if we take
f_{i}(z_{1},z_{2})
to be quotients of the integrand with
z_{i}\mapsto z_{i}+N
in the numerators, then

\displaystyle f_{1}(z_{1},z_{2})={}

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{e}((z_{1}+N)^{2}/\tau+(z_{1}+N)z_{2}/\tau+(z_{1}+N)(-m-m^{\prime}/\tau)+z_{2}(1+1/\tau))}{\Phi(z_{2};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(2z_{1}+2N;\tau)^{-1}\Phi(z_{2}-z_{1}-N;\tau)^{-1}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\Phi(z_{2};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(2z_{1};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(z_{2}-z_{1};\tau)^{-1}}{\mathbf{e}\bigl(z_{1}^{2}/\tau+z_{1}z_{2}/\tau+z_{1}(-m-m^{\prime}/\tau)+z_{2}(1+1/\tau)\bigr)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{e}(2Mz_{1}+Mz_{2})(1-\mathbf{e}(-Mz_{1}+Mz_{2}))}{(1-\mathbf{e}(2Mz_{1}))^{2}}.

Then

\displaystyle f_{2}(z_{1},z_{2})={}

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{e}(z_{1}^{2}/\tau+z_{1}(z_{2}+N)/\tau+z_{1}(-m-m^{\prime}/\tau)+(z_{2}+N)(1+1/\tau))}{\Phi(z_{2}+N;\tau)^{-1}\Phi(2z_{1};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(z_{2}+N-z_{1};\tau)^{-1}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\Phi(z_{2};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(2z_{1};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(z_{2}-z_{1};\tau)^{-1}}{\mathbf{e}\bigl(z_{1}^{2}/\tau+z_{1}z_{2}/\tau+z_{1}(-m-m^{\prime}/\tau)+z_{2}(1+1/\tau)\bigr)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{e}(Mz_{1})}{(1-\mathbf{e}(Mz_{2}))(1-\mathbf{e}(-Mz_{1}+Mz_{2}))}.

Notice that

f_{j}(z_{1}+kN,z_{2}+\ell N)=f_{j}(z_{1},z_{2}),

and therefore from a generalisation of
[
33
, Lemma 2.1]
to multiple dimensions,

\displaystyle\int_{-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}(\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0)}\int_{-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}(\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0)}\frac{\mathbf{e}\bigl(z_{1}^{2}/\tau+z_{1}z_{2}/\tau+z_{1}(-m-m^{\prime}/\tau)+z_{2}(1+1/\tau)\bigr)}{\Phi(z_{2};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(2z_{1};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(z_{2}-z_{1};\tau)^{-1}}{\rm d}z_{1}{\rm d}z_{2}

\displaystyle=\biggl(\int_{-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}(\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0)}-\int_{N-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}(\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0)}\biggr)^{2}\frac{\mathbf{e}\bigl(z_{1}^{2}/\tau+z_{1}z_{2}/\tau+z_{1}(-m-m^{\prime}/\tau)+z_{2}(1+1/\tau)\bigr){\rm d}z_{1}{\rm d}z_{2}}{\Phi(z_{2};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(2z_{1};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(z_{2}-z_{1};\tau)^{-1}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{\bigl(1-\frac{\mathbf{e}(2Mz_{1}+Mz_{2})(1-\mathbf{e}(-Mz_{1}+Mz_{2}))}{(1-\mathbf{e}(2Mz_{1}))^{2}}\bigr)\bigl(1-\frac{\mathbf{e}(Mz_{1})}{(1-\mathbf{e}(Mz_{2}))(1-\mathbf{e}(-Mz_{1}+Mz_{2}))}\bigr)}

\displaystyle=\biggl(\int_{-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}(\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0)}-\int_{N-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}(\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0)}\biggr)^{2}\frac{\mathbf{e}(z_{1}^{2}/\tau+z_{1}z_{2}/\tau+z_{1}(-m-m^{\prime}/\tau)+z_{2}(1+1/\tau))}{\Phi(z_{2};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(2z_{1};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(z_{2}-z_{1};\tau)^{-1}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{(1-\mathbf{e}(2Mz_{1}))^{2}(1-\mathbf{e}(Mz_{2}))(1-\mathbf{e}(-Mz_{1}+Mz_{2})){\rm d}z_{1}{\rm d}z_{2}}{\bigl((1-\mathbf{e}(2Mz_{1}))^{2}-\mathbf{e}(2Mz_{1}+Mz_{2})(1-\mathbf{e}(-Mz_{1}+Mz_{2}))\bigr)}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{\bigl((1-\mathbf{e}(Mz_{2}))(1-\mathbf{e}(-Mz_{1}+Mz_{2}))-\mathbf{e}(Mz_{1})\bigr)}.

(C.4)

We see that the zeros of the quantum dilogarithms now cancel with the zeros in the numerator and that the integrand has poles when

\displaystyle 0=(1-\mathbf{e}(Mz_{2}))(1-\mathbf{e}(-Mz_{1}+Mz_{2}))-\mathbf{e}(Mz_{1}),

\displaystyle 0=(1-\mathbf{e}(2Mz_{1}))^{2}-\mathbf{e}(2Mz_{1}+Mz_{2})(1-\mathbf{e}(-Mz_{1}+Mz_{2})).

Therefore, considering the algebraic variety

\displaystyle 0=(1-X_{2})\bigl(1-X_{1}^{-1}X_{2}\bigr)-X_{1},

\displaystyle 0=\bigl(1-X_{1}^{2}\bigr)^{2}-X_{1}^{2}X_{2}\bigl(1-X_{1}^{-1}X_{2}\bigr),

this can be solved exactly as notice that

\frac{1-X_{2}}{X_{1}}=\frac{X_{1}^{2}X_{2}}{\bigl(1-X_{1}^{2}\bigr)^{2}}

and so

X_{2}=\frac{\bigl(1-X_{1}^{2}\bigr)^{2}}{\bigl(1-X_{1}^{2}\bigr)^{2}+X_{1}^{3}},

and taking

\xi^{7}-\xi^{6}-2\xi^{5}+6\xi^{4}-11\xi^{3}+6\xi^{2}+3\xi-1=0,

we have

\displaystyle X_{1,j}=-3+11\xi_{j}+20\xi_{j}^{2}-15\xi_{j}^{3}+6\xi_{j}^{4}-2\xi_{j}^{5}-4\xi_{j}^{6},

\displaystyle X_{2,j}=-9+19\xi_{j}+76\xi_{j}^{2}-52\xi_{j}^{3}+20\xi_{j}^{4}-4\xi_{j}^{5}-13\xi_{j}^{6}.

There is one additional solution when
X_{1}=1
and
X_{2}=0
, however
z_{2}=\infty
for such a solution. Therefore, letting

x_{i,j,k}=\frac{1}{2\pi{\rm i}}\log(X_{i,j})+k

notice that

\displaystyle(1-\mathbf{e}(2M(z_{1}+x_{1}/M)))^{2}-\mathbf{e}(2M(z_{1}+x_{1}/M)+M(z_{2}+x_{2}/M))

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times(1-\mathbf{e}(-M(z_{1}+x_{1}/M)+M(z_{2}+x_{2}/M)))

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigl(1-X_{1}^{2}\mathbf{e}(2Mz_{1})\bigr)^{2}-X_{1}^{2}X_{2}\mathbf{e}(2Mz_{1}+Mz_{2})\bigl(1-X_{1}^{-1}X_{2}\mathbf{e}(-Mz_{1}+Mz_{2})\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigl(4X_{1}^{4}+(-2X_{2}-4)X_{1}^{2}+X_{2}^{2}X_{1}\bigr)2\pi{\rm i}Mz_{1}+\bigl(-X_{2}X_{1}^{2}+2X_{2}^{2}X_{1}\bigr)2\pi{\rm i}Mz_{2}+\cdots

and

\displaystyle\bigl((1-\mathbf{e}(M(z_{2}+x_{2}/M)))(1-\mathbf{e}(-M(z_{1}+x_{1}/M)+M(z_{2}+x_{2}/M)))-\mathbf{e}(M(z_{1}+x_{1}/M))\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigl((1-X_{2}\mathbf{e}(Mz_{2}))\bigl(1-X_{1}^{-1}X_{2}\mathbf{e}(-Mz_{1}+Mz_{2})\bigr)-X_{1}\mathbf{e}(Mz_{1})\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigl(-X_{1}+\bigl(-X_{2}^{2}+X_{2}\bigr)X_{1}^{-1}\bigr)2\pi{\rm i}Mz_{1}+\bigl(-X_{2}+\bigl(2X_{2}^{2}-X_{2}\bigr)X_{1}^{-1}\bigr)2\pi{\rm i}Mz_{2}+\cdots.

Then we can compute

\displaystyle\det\begin{pmatrix}4X_{1}^{4}+(-2X_{2}-4)X_{1}^{2}+X_{2}^{2}X_{1}&-X_{2}X_{1}^{2}+2X_{2}^{2}X_{1}\\
-X_{1}+\bigl(-X_{2}^{2}+X_{2}\bigr)X_{1}^{-1}&-X_{2}+\bigl(2X_{2}^{2}-X_{2}\bigr)X_{1}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\qquad=-257+806\xi+947\xi^{2}-749\xi^{3}+331\xi^{4}-133\xi^{5}-213\xi^{6}=\Delta.

The last ingredient we need is the quantum dilogarithm at rationals. For this, we use the cyclic dilogarithm

D_{M}(x;q)=\prod_{j=1}^{M-1}\bigl(1-q^{j}x\bigr)^{\frac{j}{M}}

and have the formula
[
33
, Theorem 1.9]

\Phi(z;\tau)^{-1}=\;\exp\biggl(\frac{1}{2\pi{\rm i}NM}\operatorname{Li}_{2}(\mathbf{e}(Mz))\biggr)(1-\mathbf{e}(Mz))^{\frac{z}{N}-1}D_{N}\bigl(\mathbf{e}(z/\tau);\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)D_{M}(\mathbf{e}(z);q).

Therefore, the double integral deformed near
(x_{1,j,k}/M,x_{2,j,\ell}/M)
gives

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{e}\bigl(x_{1,j,k}^{2}/NM+x_{1,j,k}x_{2,j,\ell}/NM+x_{1,j,k}(-m/M-m^{\prime}/N)+x_{2,j,\ell}(1/M+1/N)\bigr)}{\Phi(x_{2,j,\ell}/M+1+\tau;\tau)^{-1}\Phi(2x_{1,j,k}/M+1+\tau;\tau)^{-1}\Phi(x_{2,j,\ell}/M-x_{1,j,k}/M+1+\tau;\tau)^{-1}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\bigl(1-X_{1,j}^{2}\bigr)^{2}(1-X_{2,j})\bigl(1-X_{1,j}^{-1}X_{2,j}\bigr)}{M^{2}\Delta}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{e}(x_{1,j,k}^{2}/NM+x_{1,j,k}x_{2,j,\ell}/NM+x_{1,j,k}(-m/M-m^{\prime}/N)+x_{2,j,\ell}(1/M+1/N))}{\bigl(1-X_{1,j}^{2}\bigr)^{-2}(1-X_{2,j})^{-1}\bigl(1-X_{1,j}^{-1}X_{2,j}\bigl)^{-1}M^{2}\Delta}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\exp\Bigl(-\frac{1}{2\pi{\rm i}NM}\operatorname{Li}_{2}(X_{2,j})\Bigr)(1-X_{2,j})^{1-\frac{x_{2,j,\ell}}{NM}-\frac{1}{M}}}{D_{N}\bigl(\mathbf{e}(x_{2,j,\ell}/N);\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)D_{M}(q\mathbf{e}(x_{2,j,\ell}/M);q)}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\exp\Bigl(-\frac{1}{2\pi{\rm i}NM}\operatorname{Li}_{2}(X_{1,j}^{2})\Bigr)\bigl(1-X_{1,j}^{2}\bigr)^{1-\frac{2x_{1,j,k}}{NM}-\frac{1}{M}}}{D_{N}\bigl(\mathbf{e}(2x_{1,j,k}/N);\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)D_{M}(q\mathbf{e}(2x_{1,j,k}/M);q)}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\exp\Bigl(-\frac{1}{2\pi{\rm i}NM}\operatorname{Li}_{2}(X_{1,j}^{-1}X_{2,j})\Bigr)\bigl(1-X_{1,j}^{-1}X_{2,j}\bigr)^{1-\frac{x_{2,j,\ell}-x_{1,j,k}}{NM}-\frac{1}{M}}}{D_{N}\bigl(\mathbf{e}(x_{2,j,\ell}/N-x_{1,j,k}/N);\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)D_{M}(q\mathbf{e}(x_{2,j,\ell}/M-x_{1,j,k}/M);q)}.

Firstly, notice that

\displaystyle-\operatorname{Li}_{2}(X_{2,j})-\operatorname{Li}_{2}\bigl(X_{1,j}^{2}\bigr)-\operatorname{Li}_{2}\bigl(X_{1,j}^{-1}X_{2,j}\bigr)-2\pi{\rm i}x_{2,j,\ell}\log(1-X_{2,j})

\displaystyle-2(2\pi{\rm i})x_{1,j,k}\log\bigl(1-X_{1,j}^{2}\bigr)-2\pi{\rm i}(x_{2,j,\ell}-x_{1,j,k})\log\bigl(1-X_{1,j}^{-1}X_{2,j}\bigr)

\displaystyle+(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}x_{1,j,k}^{2}+(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}x_{1,j,k}x_{2,j,\ell}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{V}_{\rho_{j}}-4\pi^{2}\biggl(k^{2}+k\ell+a_{j}k+\frac{1}{24}\biggr),

where
a_{j}=2,0,0,1,1,-1,1
for
j=1,\dots,7
or

2\pi{\rm i}a_{j}=-2\log\bigl(1-X_{1,j}^{2}\bigr)+\log\bigl(1-X_{1,j}^{-1}X_{2,j}\bigr)+2\log(X_{1})+\log(X_{2}),

where we note that for all
j

0=-\log(1-X_{2,j})-\log\bigl(1-X_{1,j}^{-1}X_{2,j}\bigr)+\log(X_{1,j}).

Therefore, we see that

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}\bigl(x_{1,j,k}^{2}/NM+x_{1,j,k}x_{2,j,\ell}/NM\bigr)\exp\biggl(-\frac{1}{2\pi{\rm i}NM}\operatorname{Li}_{2}(X_{2,j})\biggr)(1-X_{2,j})^{-\frac{x_{2,j,\ell}}{NM}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\exp\biggl(-\frac{1}{2\pi{\rm i}NM}\operatorname{Li}_{2}\bigl(X_{1,j}^{2}\bigr)\biggr)\bigl(1-X_{1,j}^{2}\bigr)^{-\frac{2x_{1,j,k}}{NM}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\exp\biggl(-\frac{1}{2\pi{\rm i}NM}\operatorname{Li}_{2}\bigl(X_{1,j}^{-1}X_{2,j}\bigr)\biggr)\bigl(1-X_{1,j}^{-1}X_{2,j}\bigr)^{-\frac{x_{2,j,\ell}-x_{1,j,k}}{NM}}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{e}\biggl(-\frac{\mathrm{V}_{j}+\pi^{2}/6}{(2\pi{\rm i})^{2}NM}\biggr)\mathbf{e}\biggl(\frac{k^{2}+k\ell}{NM}\biggr)\mathbf{e}\biggl(\frac{ka_{j}+\ell\times 0}{NM}\biggr).

With this, using the residue and Chinese remainder theorem and the cocycle of
\varepsilon
, the final integral of equation (
C.4
) can then be shown to factorise into the bilinear sum of equation (
C.3
).
∎

Including the trivial connection, we can apply the same methods to the state integral from equation (
5.4
) written in a similar from to equation (
C.2
). This has a similar outcome with some Appell–Lerch type versions of the functions
\Sha^{(j)}_{m}(q)
of equation (
4.13
) that appear in the first column of
\mathbf{Z}_{m}(q)^{-1}
,

\displaystyle K_{n}\Sha^{(j)}_{m}(q)=\frac{-{\rm i}}{M\varepsilon(q)}\sqrt{\frac{\bigl(1-X_{1,j}^{2}\bigr)^{2}(1-X_{2,j})\bigl(1-X_{1,j}^{-1}X_{2,j}\bigr)}{\Delta_{\rho_{j}}}}\sum_{k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}/M\mathbb{Z}}\frac{q^{(n-1)m}}{\bigl(1-q^{1-n+k}X_{1,j}^{\frac{1}{m}}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\frac{q^{k^{2}+k\ell-mk+\ell}X_{1,j}^{\frac{2k+\ell-m}{M}}X_{2,j}^{\frac{k+1}{M}}\prod_{i=0}^{M-1}\bigl(1-q^{i+1+\ell-k}X_{1,j}^{-1/M}X_{2,j}^{1/M}\bigr)^{-(i+1+\ell-k)/M-1/2}}{\prod_{i=0}^{M-1}\bigl(1-q^{i+1+\ell}X_{2,j}^{1/M}\bigr)^{(i+1+\ell)/M-1/2}\prod_{i=0}^{M-1}\bigl(1-q^{i+1+2k}X_{1,j}^{2/M}\bigr)^{(i+1+2k)/M-1/2}},

so that

K_{n}\Sha^{(j)}_{m}(q)-K_{n}\Sha^{(j)}_{m-1}(q)=\Sha^{(j)}_{m}(q).

Then there are some additional residues at
z_{1}\in\tau\mathbb{Z}
, which then factorise into
\Sha_{m}(q)
and the function
1
with a shift by
\tau^{1/2}
compared to the other residues. This relies on the identity

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq\ell\leq k\leq M/2}(-1)^{k}q^{-\frac{1}{2}k(k+1)+\ell(\ell+1)+mk}\frac{(q;q)_{2k+1}}{(q;q)_{\ell}(q;q)_{k-\ell}}

\displaystyle\qquad=-\sum_{M/2\leq k\leq\ell+k\leq M-1}(-1)^{\ell}q^{k(2k+1)+k\ell+\ell(\ell+1)/2-mk-m}\frac{(q;q)_{\ell+k}}{(q;q)_{2k-M}(q;q)_{\ell}},

which follows from the fact that
(q;q)_{n}=M\bigl(q^{-1};q^{-1}\bigr)_{M-1-n}^{-1}
.

C.2
As
\boldsymbol{q}
-series

In this appendix, we will outline the proofs of Lemmas
5.2
,
5.1
and
5.5
.

Proofs of Lemma
5.2
.

The proof of equation (
5.2
) follows from automatic
q
-holomorphic proofs described in
[
74
]
. The vanishing of
Z^{(2)}_{m}(q)=0
for
|q|>1
follows from the fact that there is only one non-vanishing solution that is a power series in
q^{-m}
. Notice that
Z^{(1)}_{m}(q)
is one such solution. Therefore, the coefficient of
q^{-m}
determines
Z^{(2)}(q)
in terms of
Z^{(1)}(q)
. Therefore, for
|q|>1
we want to compute

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{j}}{(q;q)_{j}^{2}}\biggl(-\frac{1}{2}+2E_{1}(q)-2\sum_{n=1}^{j}\frac{q^{n}}{1-q^{n}}\biggr).

Considering the family,

g_{p}(q)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{j+jp}}{(q;q)_{j}^{2}}\biggl(-\frac{1}{2}-p+2E_{1}(q)-2\sum_{n=1}^{j}\frac{q^{n}}{1-q^{n}}\biggr).

This satisfies

2g_{p}(q)-(1-q^{p})g_{p-1}(q)=g_{p+1}(q).

Therefore, for
p>0
is determined by
p=0
. Moreover, we see that

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{j+jp}}{(q;q)_{j}^{2}}=\begin{cases}q^{p^{2}/4}+\cdots&\text{for }p\to\infty\text{ even},\\
2q^{(p^{2}-1)/4}+\cdots&\text{for }p\to\infty\text{ odd}.\end{cases}

satisfies the same equation. Therefore, a combination of these vanishes for all
p\geq 0
. However,

g_{p}(q)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{j+jp}}{(q;q)_{j}^{2}}\biggl(2j-p-2\sum_{n=j+1}^{n}\frac{q^{-n}}{1-q^{-n}}\biggr)

implies

g_{p}(q)=\begin{cases}o\bigl(q^{p^{2}/4}\bigr)&\text{for }p\to\infty\text{ even},\\
o\bigl(q^{(p^{2}-1)/4}\bigr)&\text{for }p\to\infty\text{ odd}.\end{cases}

Therefore, we see that
g_{p}(q)=0
for
p\geq 0
.
∎

The proof of Lemma
5.6
follows from similar methods.

Proofs of Lemma
5.1
.

From its formula in the upper half plane, we have

\displaystyle\Phi(z+m\tau+n;\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{(q\mathbf{e}(z);q)_{\infty}}{(\mathbf{e}(z/\tau);\tilde{q})_{\infty}}\frac{1}{(q\mathbf{e}(z);q)_{m}}\frac{1}{\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\mathbf{e}(z/\tau);\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(q\mathbf{e}(z);q)_{\infty}}{(\tilde{q}\mathbf{e}(z/\tau);\tilde{q})_{\infty}}\frac{1}{1-\mathbf{e}(z/\tau)}\frac{1}{(q\mathbf{e}(z);q)_{m}}\frac{1}{\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\mathbf{e}(z/\tau);\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{n}}.

We will now factorise the state integral
\mathcal{S}_{m,m^{\prime}}
.
We will integrate over
z_{2}
and then
z_{1}
by pushing the contour to infinity and collecting residues. We have

\displaystyle\int_{-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}(\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0)}\int_{-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}(\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0)}\Phi(z_{2};\tau)\Phi(2z_{1};\tau)\Phi(z_{2}-z_{1};\tau)

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathbf{e}\bigl(z_{1}^{2}/\tau+z_{1}z_{2}/\tau+z_{1}(-m-m^{\prime}/\tau)+z_{2}(1+1/\tau)\bigr){\rm d}z_{1}{\rm d}z_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}(\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0)}\!\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\!\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{z_{2}=j\tau+j^{\prime}}\!\!\frac{\mathbf{e}\bigl(2z_{1}^{2}/\tau\!+z_{1}z_{2}/\tau+z_{1}(1-m+(1-m^{\prime})/\tau)+z_{2}(1+1/\tau)\bigr)}{\Phi(z_{1}+z_{2};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(2z_{1};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(z_{2};\tau)^{-1}}{\rm d}z_{1}{\rm d}z_{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}(\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0)}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{z_{2}=j\tau+j^{\prime}}\frac{\mathbf{e}\bigl(z_{1}^{2}/\tau+z_{1}z_{2}/\tau+z_{1}(-m-m^{\prime}/\tau)+z_{2}(1+1/\tau)\bigr)}{\Phi(z_{2};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(2z_{1};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(z_{2}-z_{1};\tau)^{-1}}{\rm d}z_{1}{\rm d}z_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\tau(q;q)_{\infty}}{(\tilde{q};\tilde{q})_{\infty}}\int_{-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}(\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0)}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{j}\tilde{q}^{-j^{\prime}}\mathbf{e}\bigl(2z_{1}^{2}/\tau+z_{1}((j+1-m)\tau+(j^{\prime}+1-m^{\prime}))/\tau\bigr)}{\Phi(z_{1}+j\tau+j^{\prime};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(2z_{1};\tau)^{-1}(q;q)_{j}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{j^{\prime}}}{\rm d}z_{1}

\displaystyle-\frac{\tau(q;q)_{\infty}}{(\tilde{q};\tilde{q})_{\infty}}\int_{-{\rm i}\mathsf{b}(\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0)}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{j}\tilde{q}^{-j^{\prime}}\mathbf{e}\bigl(z_{1}^{2}/\tau+z_{1}((j-m)\tau+(j^{\prime}-m^{\prime}))/\tau\bigr)}{\Phi(2z_{1};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(j\tau+j^{\prime}-z_{1};\tau)^{-1}(q;q)_{j}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{j^{\prime}}}{\rm d}z_{1}

(C.5)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\tau(q;q)_{\infty}}{(\tilde{q};\tilde{q})_{\infty}}\sum_{k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{z_{1}=\frac{k}{2}\tau+\frac{k^{\prime}}{2}}\frac{q^{j}\tilde{q}^{-j^{\prime}}\mathbf{e}\bigl(2z_{1}^{2}/\tau+z_{1}((j+1-m)\tau+(j^{\prime}+1-m^{\prime}))/\tau\bigr)}{\Phi(z_{1}+j\tau+j^{\prime};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(2z_{1};\tau)^{-1}(q;q)_{j}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{j^{\prime}}}{\rm d}z_{1}

\displaystyle-\frac{\tau(q;q)_{\infty}}{(\tilde{q};\tilde{q})_{\infty}}\sum_{k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{z_{1}=\frac{k}{2}\tau+\frac{k^{\prime}}{2}}\frac{q^{j}\tilde{q}^{-j^{\prime}}\mathbf{e}\bigl(z_{1}^{2}/\tau+z_{1}((j-m)\tau+(j^{\prime}-m^{\prime}))/\tau\bigr)}{\Phi(2z_{1};\tau)^{-1}\Phi(j\tau+j^{\prime}-z_{1};\tau)^{-1}(q;q)_{j}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{j^{\prime}}}{\rm d}z_{1}.

We can break up these sums into congruences. In particular, we get eight sums corresponding to
k,k^{\prime}\equiv 0,1\pmod{2}
in the two sums in the last equality in equation (
C.5
). The first of the sums with
k\equiv k^{\prime}\equiv 0\pmod{2}
is given as follows:

\displaystyle\sum_{k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{z_{1}=k\tau+k^{\prime}}\frac{\Phi(z_{1}+j\tau+j^{\prime};\tau)\Phi(2z_{1};\tau)}{(q;q)_{j}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{j^{\prime}}}q^{j}\tilde{q}^{-j^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathbf{e}\bigl(2z_{1}^{2}/\tau+z_{1}((j+1-m)\tau+(j^{\prime}+1-m))/\tau\bigr){\rm d}z_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{z_{1}=0}\frac{\Phi(z_{1}+(k+j)\tau+(k^{\prime}+j^{\prime});\tau)\Phi(2z_{1}+2k\tau+2k^{\prime};\tau)}{(q;q)_{j}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{j^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathbf{e}(2(z_{1}+k\tau+k^{\prime})^{2}/\tau+(z_{1}+k\tau+k^{\prime})((j+1-m)\tau+(j^{\prime}+1-m^{\prime}))/\tau){\rm d}z_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{z_{1}=0}\frac{(q\mathbf{e}(z_{1});q)_{\infty}(q\mathbf{e}(2z_{1});q)_{\infty}q^{2k^{2}+kj+j+k-mk}\mathbf{e}((4k+j+1-m)z_{1})}{(q\mathbf{e}(z_{1});q)_{k+j}(q\mathbf{e}(2z_{1});q)_{2k}(q;q)_{j}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\mathbf{e}\bigl(8\pi{\rm i}E_{2}(q)z_{1}^{2}\bigr)(\mathbf{e}(z_{1}/\tau);\tilde{q})_{\infty}^{-1}(\mathbf{e}(2z_{1}/\tau);\tilde{q})_{\infty}^{-1}\tilde{q}^{-2k^{\prime 2}-k^{\prime}j^{\prime}-j^{\prime}-k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}k^{\prime}}}{\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\mathbf{e}(z_{1}/\tau);\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{k^{\prime}+j^{\prime}}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\mathbf{e}(2z_{1}/\tau);\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{2k^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\mathbf{e}((4k^{\prime}+j^{\prime}+1-m^{\prime})z_{1}/\tau)\mathbf{e}\bigl(8\pi{\rm i}E_{2}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)(z_{1}/\tau)^{2}\bigr)}{\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{j^{\prime}}}{\rm d}z_{1},

where we used the identity

8\pi{\rm i}E_{2}(q)-8\pi{\rm i}\tau^{-2}E_{2}(\tilde{q})=\frac{2}{\tau}.

This can now be expanded using Lemma
C.1
to find

\displaystyle\sum_{k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{z_{1}=0}\frac{q^{2k^{2}+kj+j+k-mk}\mathbf{e}((4k+j+1-m)z_{1})\mathbf{e}\bigl(8\pi iE_{2}(q)z_{1}^{2}\bigr)}{(q;q)_{k+j}(q;q)_{2k}(q;q)_{j}}

\displaystyle\times\sqrt{\frac{-(2\pi{\rm i}z_{1})}{1-\mathbf{e}(z_{1})}}\exp\left(-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(E_{\ell}(q)-\sum_{n=1}^{k+j}\operatorname{Li}_{1-\ell}(q^{n})\right)\frac{(2\pi{\rm i}z_{1})^{\ell}}{\ell!}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\sqrt{\frac{-(4\pi{\rm i}z_{1})}{1-\mathbf{e}(2z_{1})}}\exp\left(-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(E_{\ell}(q)-\sum_{n=1}^{2k}\operatorname{Li}_{1-\ell}(q^{n})\right)\frac{(4\pi{\rm i}z_{1})^{\ell}}{\ell!}\right)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\tilde{q}^{-2k^{\prime 2}-k^{\prime}j^{\prime}-j^{\prime}-k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\mathbf{e}((4k^{\prime}+j^{\prime}+1-m^{\prime})z_{1}/\tau)\mathbf{e}\bigl(8\pi{\rm i}E_{2}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)(z_{1}/\tau)^{2}\bigr)}{\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{k^{\prime}+j^{\prime}}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{2k^{\prime}}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{j^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{-1}{\sqrt{-(2\pi{\rm i}z_{1}/\tau)(1-\mathbf{e}(z_{1}/\tau))}}\exp\left(-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(E_{\ell}(\tilde{q}^{-1})-\sum_{n=1}^{k^{\prime}+j^{\prime}}\operatorname{Li}_{1-\ell}(\tilde{q}^{-n})\right)\frac{(2\pi{\rm i}z_{1}/\tau)^{\ell}}{\ell!}\right)

\displaystyle\times\frac{-1}{\sqrt{-(4\pi{\rm i}z_{1}/\tau)(1-\mathbf{e}(z_{1}/\tau))}}\exp\left(-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(E_{\ell}(\tilde{q}^{-1})-\sum_{n=1}^{2k^{\prime}}\operatorname{Li}_{1-\ell}(\tilde{q}^{-n})\right)\frac{(4\pi{\rm i}z_{1}/\tau)^{\ell}}{\ell!}\right){\rm d}z_{1}.

Considering the
q
terms expanded to second order, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k,j=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{2k^{2}+kj+j+k}}{(q;q)_{k+j}(q;q)_{2k}(q;q)_{j}}

\displaystyle\quad{}\times\exp\Bigg(\left(4k+j-m+\frac{1}{4}-3E_{1}(q)+\sum_{n=1}^{k+j}\operatorname{Li}_{0}(q^{n})+2\sum_{n=1}^{2k}\operatorname{Li}_{0}(q^{n})\right)(2\pi{\rm i}z_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\left(-\frac{5}{48}+\frac{3}{2}E_{2}(q)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n=1}^{k+j}\operatorname{Li}_{-1}(q^{n})+2\sum_{n=1}^{2k}\operatorname{Li}_{-1}(q^{n})\right)(2\pi{\rm i}z_{1})^{2}+\cdots\Bigg).

(C.6)

The second of the sums with
k\equiv k^{\prime}\equiv 0\pmod{2}
is given as follows:

\displaystyle\sum_{k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{z_{1}=k\tau+k^{\prime}}\frac{\Phi(2z_{1};\tau)\Phi(j\tau+j^{\prime}-z_{1};\tau)}{(q;q)_{j}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{j^{\prime}}}q^{j}\tilde{q}^{-j^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad{}\times\mathbf{e}\bigl(z_{1}^{2}/\tau+z_{1}((j-m)\tau+(j^{\prime}-m^{\prime}))/\tau\bigr){\rm d}z_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{z_{1}=0}\frac{\Phi(2z_{1}+2k\tau+2k^{\prime};\tau)\Phi((j-k)\tau+(j^{\prime}-k^{\prime})-z_{1};\tau)}{(q;q)_{j}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{j^{\prime}}}q^{j}\tilde{q}^{-j^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad{}\times\mathbf{e}\bigl((z_{1}+k\tau+k^{\prime})^{2}/\tau+(z_{1}+k\tau+k^{\prime})((j-m)\tau+(j^{\prime}-m^{\prime}))/\tau\bigr){\rm d}z_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k,j,k^{\prime},j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{z_{1}=0}\frac{(q\mathbf{e}(2z_{1});q)_{\infty}(q\mathbf{e}(-z_{1});q)_{\infty}q^{k^{2}+kj+j-mk}\mathbf{e}((2k+j+m)z_{1})\mathbf{e}\bigl(4\pi iE_{2}(q)z_{1}^{2}\bigr)}{(q\mathbf{e}(2z_{1});q)_{2k}(q\mathbf{e}(-z_{1});q)_{j-k}(q;q)_{j}}

\displaystyle\quad{}\times\frac{(\mathbf{e}(2z_{1}/\tau);\tilde{q})_{\infty}^{-1}(\mathbf{e}(-z_{1}/\tau);\tilde{q})_{\infty}^{-1}\tilde{q}^{-k^{\prime 2}-k^{\prime}j^{\prime}-j^{\prime}+m^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\mathbf{e}((2k^{\prime}+j^{\prime}-m^{\prime})z_{1}/\tau)}{\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\mathbf{e}(2z_{1}/\tau);\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{2k^{\prime}}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\mathbf{e}(-z_{1}/\tau);\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{j^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\mathbf{e}\bigl(4\pi{\rm i}G_{2}(\tilde{q}^{-1})(z_{1}/\tau)^{2}\bigr)}{\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{j^{\prime}}}{\rm d}z_{1}.

This can be expanded using Lemma
C.1
and on a certain cone, we find

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq j,k^{\prime}\leq j^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{z_{1}=0}\frac{q^{k^{2}+kj+j-mk}\mathbf{e}((2k+j-m)z_{1})\mathbf{e}\bigl(4\pi{\rm i}E_{2}(q)z_{1}^{2}\bigr)}{(q;q)_{2k}(q;q)_{j-k}(q;q)_{j}}

\displaystyle\times\sqrt{\frac{(2\pi{\rm i}z_{1})}{1-\mathbf{e}(-z_{1})}}\exp\left(-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(E_{\ell}(q)-\sum_{n=1}^{j-k}\operatorname{Li}_{1-\ell}(q^{n})\right)\frac{(-2\pi{\rm i}z_{1})^{\ell}}{\ell!}\right)

\displaystyle\times\sqrt{\frac{-(4\pi{\rm i}z_{1})}{1-\mathbf{e}(2z_{1})}}\exp\left(-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(E_{\ell}(q)-\sum_{n=1}^{2k}\operatorname{Li}_{1-\ell}(q^{n})\right)\frac{(4\pi{\rm i}z_{1})^{\ell}}{\ell!}\right)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\tilde{q}^{-k^{\prime 2}-k^{\prime}j^{\prime}-j^{\prime}+m^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\mathbf{e}((2k^{\prime}+j^{\prime}-m^{\prime})z_{1}/\tau)\mathbf{e}\bigl(4\pi{\rm i}G_{2}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)(z_{1}/\tau)^{2}\bigr)}{\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{2k^{\prime}}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{j^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{j^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{-1}{\sqrt{(2\pi{\rm i}z_{1}/\tau)(1-\mathbf{e}(-z_{1}/\tau))}}\exp\!\left(-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(E_{\ell}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)-\sum_{n=1}^{j^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}\operatorname{Li}_{1-\ell}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-n}\bigr)\right)\!\frac{(-2\pi{\rm i}z_{1}/\tau)^{\ell}}{\ell!}\right)

\displaystyle\times\frac{-1}{\sqrt{-(4\pi{\rm i}z_{1}/\tau)(1-\mathbf{e}(2z_{1}/\tau))}}\exp\!\left(-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(E_{\ell}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)-\sum_{n=1}^{2k^{\prime}}\operatorname{Li}_{1-\ell}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-n}\bigr)\right)\!\frac{(4\pi{\rm i}z_{1}/\tau)^{\ell}}{\ell!}\right){\rm d}z_{1}.

Considering the
q
terms and expanding to second order, we find

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq j=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{k^{2}+kj+j-mk}}{(q;q)_{2k}(q;q)_{j-k}(q;q)_{j}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\exp\Bigg(\left(2k+j-m-\frac{1}{4}-E_{1}(q)-\sum_{n=1}^{j-k}\operatorname{Li}_{0}(q^{n})+2\sum_{n=1}^{2k}\operatorname{Li}_{-1}(q^{n})\right)(2\pi{\rm i}z_{1})

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\left(-\frac{5}{48}-\frac{1}{2}E_{2}(q)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n=1}^{j-k}\operatorname{Li}_{-1}(q^{n})+2\sum_{n=1}^{2k}\operatorname{Li}_{-1}(q^{n})\right)(2\pi{\rm i}z_{1})^{2}+\cdots\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k,j=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{2k^{2}+kj+j+k-mk}}{(q;q)_{2k}(q;q)_{j}(q;q)_{j+k}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\exp\Bigg(\left(3k+j-m-\frac{1}{4}-E_{1}(q)-\sum_{n=1}^{j}\operatorname{Li}_{0}(q^{n})+2\sum_{n=1}^{2k}\operatorname{Li}_{-1}(q^{n})\right)(2\pi{\rm i}z_{1})

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\left(-\frac{5}{48}-\frac{1}{2}E_{2}(q)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n=1}^{j}\operatorname{Li}_{-1}(q^{n})+2\sum_{n=1}^{2k}\operatorname{Li}_{-1}(q^{n})\right)(2\pi{\rm i}z_{1})^{2}+\cdots\Bigg).

(C.7)

On another cone, we find

\displaystyle\sum_{j<k,j^{\prime}<k^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{z_{1}=0}\frac{q^{k^{2}+kj+j-mk}\mathbf{e}((2k+j-m)z_{1})\mathbf{e}\bigl(4\pi{\rm i}E_{2}(q)z_{1}^{2}\bigr)\bigl(q^{-1};q^{-1}\bigr)_{k-j-1}}{(q;q)_{2k}(q;q)_{j}}

\displaystyle\quad\times-\sqrt{(2\pi{\rm i}z_{1})(1-\mathbf{e}(-z_{1}))}\exp\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(E_{\ell}\bigl(q^{-1}\bigr)-\sum_{n=1}^{k-j-1}\operatorname{Li}_{1-\ell}(q^{-n})\right)\frac{(-2\pi{\rm i}z_{1})^{\ell}}{\ell!}\right)

\displaystyle\times\sqrt{\frac{-(4\pi{\rm i}z_{1})}{1-\mathbf{e}(2z_{1})}}\exp\left(-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(E_{\ell}(q)-\sum_{n=1}^{2k}\operatorname{Li}_{1-\ell}(q^{n})\right)\frac{(4\pi{\rm i}z_{1})^{\ell}}{\ell!}\right)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\tilde{q}^{-k^{\prime 2}-k^{\prime}j^{\prime}-j^{\prime}+m^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\mathbf{e}((2k^{\prime}+j^{\prime}-m^{\prime})z_{1}/\tau)\mathbf{e}\bigl(4\pi{\rm i}G_{2}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)(z_{1}/\tau)^{2}\bigr)(\tilde{q};\tilde{q})_{k^{\prime}-j^{\prime}-1}}{\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{2k^{\prime}}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1};\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)_{j^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\times\sqrt{\frac{1-\mathbf{e}(z_{1}/\tau)}{-(2\pi{\rm i}z_{1}/\tau)}}\exp\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(E_{\ell}(\tilde{q})-\sum_{n=1}^{k^{\prime}-j^{\prime}-1}\operatorname{Li}_{1-\ell}(\tilde{q}^{n})\right)\frac{(2\pi{\rm i}z_{1}/\tau)^{\ell}}{\ell!}\right)

\displaystyle\times\frac{-1}{\sqrt{-(4\pi{\rm i}z_{1}/\tau)(1-\mathbf{e}(2z_{1}/\tau))}}\exp\left(-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\left(E_{\ell}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)-\sum_{n=1}^{2k^{\prime}}\operatorname{Li}_{1-\ell}(\tilde{q}^{-n})\right)\frac{(4\pi{\rm i}z_{1}/\tau)^{\ell}}{\ell!}\right){\rm d}z_{1},

and so taking the
q
terms and expanding to first order, we find that

\displaystyle-(2\pi{\rm i}z_{1})\sum_{j<k=0}^{\infty}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{z_{1}=0}\frac{q^{k^{2}+kj+j-mk}\bigl(q^{-1};q^{-1}\bigr)_{k-j-1}}{(q;q)_{2k}(q;q)_{j}}

(C.8)

\displaystyle\times\exp\left(\left(2k+j-m-3/4-E_{1}(q)+2\sum_{n=1}^{2k}\operatorname{Li}_{0}(q^{n})+\sum_{n=1}^{k-j-1}\operatorname{Li}_{0}(q^{-n})\right)(2\pi{\rm i}z_{1})+\cdots\right).

Therefore, the contribution of the sums with
k\equiv k^{\prime}\equiv 0\pmod{2}
to the residue is given by

Z_{m}^{(1)}(q)Z_{m^{\prime}}^{(2)}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)+\tau Z_{m}^{(2)}(q)Z_{m^{\prime}}^{(1)}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr).

When we restrict to congruences when
k
or
k^{\prime}
are odd we find simple poles and the computation is much simpler. There it is not hard to see the bilinear combinations of the series
Z^{(4)}_{m}(q),\dots,Z^{(9)}_{m}(q)
.
∎

Proofs of Lemma
5.5
.

To factorise the state integral of equation (
5.4
), we apply the same kind of computations to find Appell–Lerch type sums in
\tilde{q}^{-1}
to find Lemma
5.5
. This lemma is written in terms of the following explicit
q
-series.

For
n>1
, we find

\displaystyle K^{(1)}_{m,n}(q)=(q;q)_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{k,j=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{k(2k+1)+jk+j-mk+(n-1)m}}{(q;q)_{j}(q;q)_{2k}(q;q)_{k+j}\bigl(1-q^{1-n+k}\bigr)},

\displaystyle K^{(2)}_{m,n}(q)=(q;q)_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{k,j=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{k(2k+1)+jk+j-mk+(n-1)m}}{(q;q)_{j}(q;q)_{2k}(q;q)_{k+j}\bigl(1-q^{1-n+k}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\hphantom{K^{(2)}_{m,n}(q)=}{}\times\left(-k-\frac{1}{2}+2E_{1}(q)-\sum_{\ell=1}^{j}\frac{q^{\ell}}{1-q^{\ell}}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k+j}\frac{q^{\ell}}{1-q^{\ell}}\right)

\displaystyle\hphantom{K^{(2)}_{m,n}(q)=}{}+(q;q)_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=-k}^{-1}\frac{q^{k(2k+1)+jk+j-mk+(n-1)m}\bigl(q^{-1};q^{-1}\bigr)_{-j-1}}{(q;q)_{2k}(q;q)_{k+j}\bigl(1-q^{1-n+k}\bigr)},

\displaystyle K^{(4)}_{m,n}(q)=(q;q)_{\infty}\bigl(q^{3/2};q\bigr)_{\infty}\sum_{k,j=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{(2k+1)(2k+2)/2+(k+1/2)j-m(k+1/2)+j+(n-1)m}}{(q;q)_{j}(q;q)_{2k+1}\bigl(q^{3/2};q\bigr)_{k+j}\bigl(1-q^{3/2-n+k}\bigr)},

\displaystyle K^{(5)}_{m,n}(q)=(q;q)_{\infty}(-q;q)_{\infty}\sum_{k,j=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{j+m}\frac{q^{k(2k+1)+jk+j-mk+(n-1)m}}{(q;q)_{j}(q;q)_{2k}(-q;q)_{k+j}\bigl(1+q^{1-n+k}\bigr)},

\displaystyle K^{(6)}_{m,n}(q)=(q;q)_{\infty}\bigl(-q^{3/2};q\bigr)_{\infty}\sum_{k,j=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{j+m}\frac{q^{(2k+1)(2k+2)/2+(k+1/2)j-m(k+1/2)+j+(n-1)m}}{(q;q)_{j}(q;q)_{2k+1}\bigl(-q^{3/2};q\bigr)_{k+j}\bigl(1+q^{3/2-n+k}\bigr)},

\displaystyle K^{(7)}_{m,n}(q)=(q;q)_{\infty}\bigl(q^{1/2};q\bigr)_{\infty}

\displaystyle\hphantom{K^{(7)}_{m,n}(q)=}{}\times\sum_{k,j=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{(2k+1)(2k+2)/2+(j-k-1/2)(k+1/2)+(j-k-1/2)-m(k+1/2)+(n-1)m}}{\bigl(q^{1/2};q\bigr)_{j-k}(q;q)_{2k+1}(q;q)_{j}\bigl(1-q^{3/2-n+k}\bigr)},

\displaystyle K^{(8)}_{m,n}(q)=(q;q)_{\infty}(-q;q)_{\infty}\sum_{k,j=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{j+m}\frac{q^{k(2k+1)+(j-k)k+(j-k)-mk+(n-1)m}}{(-q;q)_{j-k}(q;q)_{2k}(q;q)_{j}\bigl(1+q^{1-n+k}\bigr)},

\displaystyle K^{(9)}_{m,n}(q)=(q;q)_{\infty}\bigl(-q^{1/2};q\bigr)_{\infty}

(C.9)

\displaystyle\phantom{K^{(9)}_{m,n}(q)=}{}\times\sum_{k,j=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{j+m}\frac{q^{(2k+1)(2k+2)/2+(j-k-1/2)(k+1/2)+(j-k-1/2)-m(k+1/2)+(n-1)m}}{\bigl(-q^{1/2};q\bigr)_{j-k}(q;q)_{2k+1}(q;q)_{j}\bigl(1+q^{3/2-n+k}\bigr)},

and for
n=1
, we have

\displaystyle K^{(1)}_{m,1}(q)=(q;q)_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{k(2k+1)+jk+j-mk}}{(q;q)_{j}(q;q)_{2k}(q;q)_{k+j}(1-q^{k})}

\displaystyle\hphantom{K^{(1)}_{m,1}(q)=}{}+(q;q)_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{j}}{(q;q)_{j}^{2}}\left(m-\frac{1}{2}+2E_{1}(q)-2\sum_{n=1}^{j}\frac{q^{n}}{1-q^{n}}\right),

\displaystyle K^{(2)}_{m,1}(q)=(q;q)_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{k(2k+1)+jk+j-mk}}{(q;q)_{j}(q;q)_{2k}(q;q)_{k+j}(1-q^{k})}

\displaystyle\hphantom{K^{(2)}_{m,1}(q)=}{}\times\left(-k-\frac{1}{2}+2E_{1}(q)-\sum_{\ell=1}^{j}\frac{q^{\ell}}{1-q^{\ell}}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k+j}\frac{q^{\ell}}{1-q^{\ell}}\right)

\displaystyle\hphantom{K^{(2)}_{m,1}(q)=}{}+(q;q)_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{j=-k}^{-1}\frac{q^{k(2k+1)+jk+j-mk}\bigl(q^{-1};q^{-1}\bigr)_{-j-1}}{(q;q)_{2k}(q;q)_{k+j}(1-q^{k})}

\displaystyle\hphantom{K^{(2)}_{m,1}(q)=}{}+(q;q)_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{j}}{(q;q)_{j}^{2}}\biggl(\!\bigl(j-m-2E_{1}(q)\bigr)\!\biggl(\frac{1}{2}-2E_{1}(q)+2\sum_{\ell=1}^{j}\frac{q^{\ell}}{1-q^{\ell}}\biggr)\!+2E_{2}(q)\!\biggr).

Using these functions and elementary
q
-holonomic methods, one can prove that for
j=1,2,4,5,\allowbreak 6,7,8,9
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2q(1-q)}L^{(j)}_{m}(q)={}

\displaystyle q^{3}K^{(j)}_{-m-6,3}(q)+q^{2}K^{(j)}_{-m-5,3}(q)+\left(\frac{1}{2}+q\right)K^{(j)}_{-m-5,2}(q)

\displaystyle+\bigl(-q^{-1}-1\bigr)K^{(j)}_{-m-4,3}(q)+K^{(j)}_{-m-4,2}(q)+\frac{1}{2}q^{-1}K^{(j)}_{-m-4,1}(q)

\displaystyle+\bigl(-q^{-2}-q^{-1}\bigr)K^{(j)}_{-m-3,3}(q)+\frac{1}{2}q^{-2}K^{(j)}_{-m-3,2}(q)

\displaystyle+q^{-4}K^{(j)}_{-m-2,3}(q)+q^{-3}K^{(j)}_{-m-2,2}(q)+q^{-5}K^{(j)}_{-m-1,3}(q).

(C.10)

Then to finish the proof consider

\displaystyle\frac{2-2\tilde{q}}{q^{m}\tilde{q}^{2-m^{\prime}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0}\int_{\mathbb{R}-{\rm i}0}\Phi_{\mathsf{b}}(x_{2}+c_{\mathsf{b}})\Phi_{\mathsf{b}}(2x_{1}+c_{\mathsf{b}})\Phi_{\mathsf{b}}(x_{2}-x_{1}+c_{\mathsf{b}})

\displaystyle\qquad{}\times\mathbf{e}\bigl(-x_{1}^{2}-x_{1}x_{2}+ix_{1}(m\mathsf{b}-m^{\prime}\mathsf{b}^{-1})-{\rm i}x_{2}\bigl(\mathsf{b}+\mathsf{b}^{-1}\bigr)\bigr)\frac{1}{1-\mathbf{e}\bigl(-{\rm i}x_{1}\mathsf{b}^{-1}\bigr)\tilde{q}^{n}}{\rm d}x_{1}{\rm d}x_{2}.

Has a similar residue formula as that in equation (
C.5
). Then using the expansions in equation (
C.6
), equation (
C.7
), and equation (
C.8
) we see that the corresponding congruence sums with
k\equiv k^{\prime}\equiv 0\pmod{2}
leads to a contribution

Z_{m}^{(1)}(q)K_{m^{\prime},n}^{(2)}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)+\tau Z_{m}^{(2)}(q)K_{m^{\prime},n}^{(1)}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)+\tau^{2}Z_{m}^{(3)}(q)K_{m^{\prime},n}^{(0)}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr).

Then the odd congruences give the other contributions

\displaystyle Z_{m}^{(4)}(q)K_{m^{\prime},n}^{(5)}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)+Z_{m}^{(5)}(q)K_{m^{\prime},n}^{(4)}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)+Z_{m}^{(6)}(q)K_{m^{\prime},n}^{(6)}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+Z_{m}^{(7)}(q)K_{m^{\prime},n}^{(8)}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)+Z_{m}^{(8)}(q)K_{m^{\prime},n}^{(7)}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr)+Z_{m}^{(9)}(q)K_{m^{\prime},n}^{(9)}\bigl(\tilde{q}^{-1}\bigr).

Combining these into the full integral in equation (
5.4
) giving
\mathcal{S}^{(0)}_{m,m^{\prime}}(\tau)
and using equation (
C.10
) completes the proof.
∎

D.2
Generating series of Stokes constants

The conjectural generating series of Stokes constants for the series
\Phi^{(\rho_{0})}
of
4_{1}(-1,2)
are given

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{+}^{(\rho_{1},\rho_{0})}(q)={}

\displaystyle-q^{3}-3q^{4}-4q^{5}-4q^{6}-2q^{7}+3q^{8}+9q^{9}+18q^{10}+27q^{11}+36q^{12}+41q^{13}

\displaystyle+38q^{14}+29q^{15}+3q^{16}-35q^{17}-92q^{18}-163q^{19}-252q^{20}+\cdots,

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{+}^{(\rho_{2},\rho_{0})}(q)={}

\displaystyle-1+q^{3}+3q^{4}+q^{5}-5q^{7}-9q^{8}-15q^{9}-20q^{10}-26q^{11}-24q^{12}-19q^{13}

\displaystyle-6q^{14}+20q^{15}+53q^{16}+101q^{17}+155q^{18}+216q^{19}+274q^{20}+\cdots,

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{+}^{(\rho_{3},\rho_{0})}(q)={}

\displaystyle-q+q^{2}-q^{4}-q^{5}-2q^{6}-q^{7}-q^{8}+4q^{9}+6q^{10}+12q^{11}+15q^{12}+21q^{13}

\displaystyle+18q^{14}+19q^{15}+8q^{16}-5q^{17}-31q^{18}-59q^{19}-99q^{20}+\cdots,

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{+}^{(\rho_{4},\rho_{0})}(q)={}

\displaystyle-q-q^{2}+q^{3}+q^{4}+4q^{5}+3q^{6}+3q^{7}-8q^{9}-14q^{10}-25q^{11}-35q^{12}

\displaystyle-45q^{13}-45q^{14}-46q^{15}-26q^{16}+50q^{18}+113q^{19}+200q^{20}+\cdots,

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{+}^{(\rho_{5},\rho_{0})}(q)={}

\displaystyle-2q^{2}-2q^{3}-2q^{4}+q^{6}+6q^{7}+10q^{8}+15q^{9}+19q^{10}+20q^{11}+15q^{12}

\displaystyle+3q^{13}-13q^{14}-47q^{15}-81q^{16}-129q^{17}-176q^{18}-227q^{19}-261q^{20}+\cdots,

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{+}^{(\rho_{6},\rho_{0})}(q)={}

\displaystyle 2q^{2}+q^{3}-5q^{5}-6q^{6}-12q^{7}-13q^{8}-12q^{9}-6q^{10}+3q^{11}+25q^{12}+51q^{13}

\displaystyle+78q^{14}+117q^{15}+144q^{16}+171q^{17}+173q^{18}+157q^{19}+96q^{20}+\cdots,

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{+}^{(\rho_{7},\rho_{0})}(q)={}

\displaystyle q+2q^{2}+q^{3}+q^{4}-4q^{5}-7q^{6}-13q^{7}-15q^{8}-13q^{9}-9q^{10}+5q^{11}+26q^{12}

\displaystyle+56q^{13}+86q^{14}+132q^{15}+161q^{16}+193q^{17}+196q^{18}+182q^{19}

\displaystyle+114q^{20}+\cdots,

and

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{-}^{(\rho_{1},\rho_{0})}(q)={}

\displaystyle-1+q+3q^{2}+2q^{3}+q^{4}-q^{5}-2q^{6}-10q^{7}-15q^{8}-21q^{9}-28q^{10}-28q^{11}

\displaystyle-20q^{12}-5q^{13}+22q^{14}+65q^{15}+116q^{16}+174q^{17}+239q^{18}+304q^{19}

\displaystyle+353q^{20}+\cdots,

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{-}^{(\rho_{2},\rho_{0})}(q)={}

\displaystyle q-q^{2}-3q^{3}-q^{4}-3q^{5}+q^{6}+q^{7}+6q^{8}+14q^{9}+19q^{10}+27q^{11}+31q^{12}

\displaystyle+28q^{13}+18q^{14}-37q^{16}-77q^{17}-137q^{18}-200q^{19}-272q^{20}+\cdots,

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{-}^{(\rho_{3},\rho_{0})}(q)={}

\displaystyle-1+2q+q^{2}+q^{3}-q^{5}-2q^{6}-6q^{7}-8q^{8}-13q^{9}-14q^{10}-14q^{11}-5q^{12}

\displaystyle-q^{13}+20q^{14}+39q^{15}+69q^{16}+96q^{17}+133q^{18}+157q^{19}+184q^{20}+\cdots,

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{-}^{(\rho_{4},\rho_{0})}(q)={}

\displaystyle 1-2q-q^{2}-q^{3}-q^{4}+2q^{5}+3q^{6}+10q^{7}+12q^{8}+17q^{9}+18q^{10}+16q^{11}

\displaystyle+3q^{12}-11q^{13}-41q^{14}-76q^{15}-117q^{16}-159q^{17}-204q^{18}-232q^{19}

\displaystyle-249q^{20}+\cdots,

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{-}^{(\rho_{5},\rho_{0})}(q)={}

\displaystyle q-q^{2}-2q^{3}-3q^{4}-3q^{5}-q^{6}-q^{7}+6q^{8}+11q^{9}+21q^{10}+31q^{11}+40q^{12}
